From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because I finally got scared enough to make an appointment. It's just... lately, after I eat too much, I've been going into the bathroom and making myself throw up. I just don't want to gain weight, you know? But it's not just a one-time mistake anymore, it's happening more often. \n\nIt reminds me of this time back when I first started working at the department store. We had this huge holiday sale, and everyone was bringing in baked goods for the breakroom. I swear, there were cookies and fudge everywhere. My manager, Brenda—she was this older lady who always wore too much perfume—she brought in this incredible pecan pie. I must have eaten three slices. Anyway, the store was so busy that day, people were practically fighting over the last cashmere sweaters. I remember this one customer actually yelled at me because we didn't have her size in navy blue. Can you believe that? People get so crazy around the holidays. But yeah, that's what's been happening with the bathroom and the throwing up.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, terrified. I really am. It’s like... if my skirt feels even a little bit tight when I'm getting dressed for church, or if I happen to catch my side view in one of the big display mirrors at the store, it just ruins my entire day. I start feeling like everyone is looking at me and they can all tell I’ve just let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of church, actually, our pastor—Pastor Miller—he just retired last month after twenty years. We had this huge potluck for him down in the fellowship hall. The basement there always smells a little damp, you know? Like old paper and mildew. But Maryanne brought her famous potato salad, and I swear she used way too much paprika this time. She’s been so distracted since her daughter moved to Ohio. I just can't imagine living somewhere with winters that bad, the snow would drive me crazy. I've always said I need the sun. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, right, yes. The mirrors. I just panic about how big I look. So yes, I'm very afraid of gaining weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is actually a problem right now?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's a *problem* exactly, but I definitely think I'm too heavy. I just feel so... bulky. Especially around my middle. I tried buying this new dress for my niece's wedding last week—she's marrying this boy she met at college, an engineering student, I think? Or maybe architecture. Anyway, they're having a big outdoor wedding in the spring, which I told my sister is a terrible idea because you just can't trust the weather in April. Remember that year we got a blizzard on Easter weekend? We had to cancel the whole egg hunt at the park, and the kids were just devastated. My grandson cried for an hour because he'd been practicing finding the golden egg. He's such a sweet boy, always helping his mother in the garden. They grow the most beautiful tomatoes...\n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think my weight is a medical problem or anything, I just... I just know I need to lose some. It's just not where I want it to be.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, for at least the past several months... it’s been happening most weeks. I'll have one of those secret eating spells, usually after I get off work. I always stop by the grocery store on my way home—the big one over on Maple Street. They just completely changed the layout in there, and let me tell you, it makes absolutely no sense. They moved the bakery section all the way to the back, right past the pharmacy. \n\nI asked the manager about it—this young kid, couldn't have been more than twenty—and he just looked at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days just don't have the customer service skills we had to learn. When I started in retail, if you didn't smile and walk a customer directly to the item they were looking for, you were written up on the spot. Now they just point vaguely and go right back to staring at their phones. It's a shame, really. My neighbor's boy works at the hardware store down the road and she says he's the exact same way. He didn't even know what a Phillips head screwdriver was when he started! Can you imagine? \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right. So I'll get all this food from the store, and then I go home and eat it all in secret, and then I go make myself sick afterward. So yes, it's most weeks now. I can’t honestly call it occasional anymore, as much as I'd like to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to lose weight if everyone around you told you that you look fine and aren't too heavy?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think it would matter what anyone else said. I'd still want to lose it. It's just how I feel when I look in the mirror, you know? It's not about them, it's about me. \n\nMy sister, actually, she's always telling me I look fine. But she says that to everyone. She's just one of those people who can't stand conflict. We were at the mall last week—the one out by the highway, not the one downtown, they practically boarded that one up after the anchor stores left. Anyway, we were looking for a gift for our cousin's baby shower. They're having a little girl, which is nice because the last three babies in the family have been boys. My nephew's youngest, little Tommy, he is just a terror. He broke a lamp at Thanksgiving and didn't even apologize. His mother just laughed it off! I swear, nobody disciplines their children anymore. \n\nWhen my kids were little, if they had broken a lamp at someone else's house, they would have been doing chores for a month to pay for it. We had this beautiful ceramic lamp in the living room, painted with these little bluebirds. I got it at an estate sale down in Florida when we went to visit my in-laws. Oh, the humidity down there is just unbearable in August. You step outside and your glasses immediately fog up. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, right. If people told me I looked fine. No, it wouldn't change anything. I just... I just know I need to be smaller.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look different from how you actually appear in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I see something completely different from what's there, but... I do know that how I look in the mirror really dictates how I feel. Like, if my skirt feels tight before church on Sunday, or if I catch my side view in one of those big mirrors at the store—they have those huge ones by the fitting rooms now, the lighting is just awful, fluorescent bulbs that make everyone look washed out. \n\nI told the district manager about the lighting when he came for a visit last month. He's this tall man, always wears these very sharp suits, probably imported. He brought his wife in once, and she had the most beautiful leather handbag. I think it was Italian. I've always wanted to go to Italy. My friend Susan went to Tuscany for her anniversary, and she said the pasta there is nothing like what we have here. They make it fresh every day, right in front of you. She brought me back this little bottle of olive oil, and it was so rich and green... \n\nBut anyway, if I see myself in one of those mirrors and I look heavy, or my clothes feel tight, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go, and I just can't stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look fat even when you're at a weight that most people would consider normal?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose so. Even if the doctor says my weight is fine, if my skirt feels tight before church on Sunday, or if I catch my side view in one of those awful store mirrors, it just completely ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of church, they just got a new choir director. The old one, Mr. Henderson, retired after thirty years. He had the most wonderful baritone voice. The new fellow is very young, right out of college, I think. He wants us to sing all these modern hymns with guitars and drums. Guitars in a sanctuary! It just doesn't feel right to me. I told my friend Martha—she sits two pews behind me, always wears those lovely silk scarves—I told her that next they'll be bringing coffee cups right into the service. \n\nMartha makes the most incredible pecan pie for the church bake sale every year. Her secret is a dash of bourbon in the filling. She won't give the recipe to anyone, not even her own daughter-in-law. Although, between you and me, her daughter-in-law couldn't bake a potato if her life depended on it. She tried to bring a store-bought angel food cake to the potluck last month and just put it on a nice plate. As if we wouldn't know! \n\nBut... oh, what was I saying? Right, about feeling fat. Yes, if my clothes feel even a little snug, or I see myself and think I look heavy, I just can't shake it. It doesn't matter what the scale says or what anyone else considers normal. I just feel huge.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would see yourself as fat even if you were underweight?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I honestly don't know if I'd ever be underweight, but I suppose even if I was, it wouldn't change how I feel. Like I said before, it really just comes down to how my clothes fit or what I see in the mirror. If my skirt feels tight before church, or if I catch my side view in one of those awful store mirrors, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of the store, they just changed our break room coffee machine to one of those pod things. It's supposed to be more convenient, but the coffee tastes like burnt plastic, if you ask me. And the little plastic cups just seem so wasteful! I was talking to Brenda about it—she works in shoes, her husband is a plumber and they just put in a new patio out back—and she said her daughter uses a reusable pod at home. But who has the time to wash out a tiny little mesh filter every time they want a cup of coffee? \n\nMy husband used to make a whole pot of Folgers every single morning. He’d wake up at 5 AM, rain or shine. The smell would wake me up right before my alarm went off. We had this old percolator that made this rhythmic bubbling sound. It’s funny the things you miss. He passed away five years ago this November. November is such a dreary month anyway, all the leaves are gone and it's just gray... \n\nBut, oh, I'm sorry, I'm rambling again. What were we talking about? Oh, right, being underweight. Yes, I think I'd still feel the same. It's just... the feeling of being heavy, it doesn't go away just because a number on a scale changes. If I feel big, I feel big, and that's all there is to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your body shape and weight is accurate?\n\nPatient: Accurate? Well, I'm not sure what you mean by accurate, exactly. I know I sound like a broken record here, but like I was saying before, it really just comes down to how I feel in my clothes. If I put on my skirt for church and it’s digging into my waist, or if I accidentally see my profile in those awful store mirrors, my whole day is just ruined. I feel so huge, and I just know everyone is looking at me thinking I’ve completely let myself go. \n\nI actually bought that skirt at a little shop downtown that isn't even there anymore. It's a bakery now. Helen, the lady who opened it, used to be a teller at my bank. Just up and quit one day to bake sourdough! Can you imagine taking a risk like that at our age? Giving up a steady paycheck and a pension? My husband worked for the county for thirty-five years, and his pension has been an absolute godsend since he passed. He was always so careful with our finances. He kept this little green ledger in his breast pocket and wrote down every single penny we spent. Even if we just bought a newspaper or a pack of gum, out came the little green book... \n\nBut, oh, I'm sorry, I've lost the thread again. You were asking if I see myself accurately. I suppose I don't know what's accurate to other people and what isn't. I just know that when I look in the mirror and feel heavy, it feels completely real to me, and that's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still make yourself throw up after eating if you didn't feel like you were getting fat?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose not. The whole reason I do it is because of the weight. That's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight, and... well, it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening more often. \n\nIt reminds me of when my sister, Carol, started hoarding those porcelain thimbles. At first, she just bought one on a trip to Branson, Missouri. We went there for a long weekend, took a bus tour. The driver was a hoot, he kept telling these awful knock-knock jokes the whole way down. We stayed at a motel that had a pool shaped like a guitar, which I thought was a bit tacky, but Carol loved it. Anyway, she bought one thimble with a little painted cardinal on it. He was all bark and no bite, that man. \n\nBut... oh, goodness, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm sorry. You were asking about the throwing up. Yes, if I didn't feel like I was getting fat, I wouldn't do it. It's just... The throwing up is just... it's what I do because I feel so awful about my weight. Even if I stopped doing that, I'd still be constantly worried about how I look. Like I keep saying, it just takes one little thing—if my skirt feels tight before church, or if I catch my side view in one of those horrible store mirrors, it can just ruin my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go.\n\nSpeaking of church, Pastor Thomas gave a sermon last week about gluttony, and I just wanted to sink into the floor. He's a wonderful speaker, though. He used to be a youth minister down in Florida before he came to us. His wife, Sarah, plays the organ, and she has the most beautiful singing voice. It’s my mother’s recipe, actually. She used to make it for every holiday. Thanksgiving, Christmas, Easter... she even made it for the Fourth of July once, which I thought was a bit odd for a summer barbecue, but everyone ate it anyway. \n\nBut... oh dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? The porter was this lovely man named George who brought us extra pillows. Anyway, Arthur would sit on the back porch every evening after dinner and smoke that pipe. Arthur said it made him feel silly, but he didn't smoke for a whole week after that. \n\nOh, listen to me, rattling on about Arthur's pipe. I'm sorry, Doctor. Like I was saying, what finally scared me into making this appointment is that after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight. The man who ran it, Mr. Henderson, always wore these bright red suspenders and a straw hat. He had a three-legged dog named Buster that used to sleep under the cash register. Buster lost his leg in a tractor accident, poor thing, but he hopped around just fine. Anyway, Linda and I ate grapefruit for breakfast, lunch, and dinner. But yes, if I could just lose the weight normally, I wouldn't be throwing up. It's just the only way I know how to fix it after I've eaten too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your desire to be thinner is more about achieving a certain weight or about achieving a certain body shape?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it's really about both, isn't it? The weight and the shape. It's just... like I was saying earlier, it's those little things that set me off. We had this beautiful ceramic lamp in the living room, painted with these little bluebirds. I got it at an estate sale down in Florida when we went to visit my in-laws. Oh, the humidity down there is just unbearable in August. You step outside and your glasses immediately fog up. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, right. If people told me I looked fine. No, it wouldn't change anything. I just... I just know I need to be smaller.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look different from how you actually appear in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I see something completely different from what's there, but... I do know that how I look in the mirror really dictates how I feel. Like, if my skirt feels tight before church on Sunday, or if I catch my side view in one of those big mirrors at the store—they have those huge ones by the fitting rooms now, the lighting is just awful, fluorescent bulbs that make everyone look washed out. \n\nI told the district manager about the lighting when he came for a visit last month. He's this tall man, always wears these very sharp suits, probably imported. He brought his wife in once, and she had the most beautiful leather handbag. I think it was Italian. I've always wanted to go to Italy. My friend Susan went to Tuscany for her anniversary, and she said the pasta there is nothing like what we have here. They make it fresh every day, right in front of you. She brought me back this little bottle of olive oil, and it was so rich and green... \n\nBut anyway, if I see myself in one of those mirrors and I look heavy, or my clothes feel tight, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go, and I just can't stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look fat even when you're at a weight that most people would consider normal?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose so. Even if the doctor says my weight is fine, if my skirt feels tight before church on Sunday, or if I catch my side view in one of those awful store mirrors, it just completely ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of church, they just got a new choir director. The old one, Mr. Henderson, retired after thirty years. He had the most wonderful baritone voice. The new fellow is very young, right out of college, I think. He wants us to sing all these modern hymns with guitars and drums. Guitars in a sanctuary! It just doesn't feel right to me. I told my friend Martha—she sits two pews behind me, always wears those lovely silk scarves—I told her that next they'll be bringing coffee cups right into the service. \n\nMartha makes the most incredible pecan pie for the church bake sale every year. Her secret is a dash of bourbon in the filling. She won't give the recipe to anyone, not even her own daughter-in-law. Although, between you and me, her daughter-in-law couldn't bake a potato if her life depended on it. She tried to bring a store-bought angel food cake to the potluck last month and just put it on a nice plate. As if we wouldn't know! \n\nBut... oh, what was I saying? Right, about feeling fat. Yes, if my clothes feel even a little snug, or I see myself and think I look heavy, I just can't shake it. It doesn't matter what the scale says or what anyone else considers normal. I just feel huge.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would see yourself as fat even if you were underweight?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I honestly don't know if I'd ever be underweight, but I suppose even if I was, it wouldn't change how I feel. Like I said before, it really just comes down to how my clothes fit or what I see in the mirror. If my skirt feels tight before church, or if I catch my side view in one of those awful store mirrors, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of the store, they just changed our break room coffee machine to one of those pod things. It's supposed to be more convenient, but the coffee tastes like burnt plastic, if you ask me. And the little plastic cups just seem so wasteful! I was talking to Brenda about it—she works in shoes, her husband is a plumber and they just put in a new patio out back—and she said her daughter uses a reusable pod at home. But who has the time to wash out a tiny little mesh filter every time they want a cup of coffee? \n\nMy husband used to make a whole pot of Folgers every single morning. He’d wake up at 5 AM, rain or shine. The smell would wake me up right before my alarm went off. We had this old percolator that made this rhythmic bubbling sound. It’s funny the things you miss. He passed away five years ago this November. November is such a dreary month anyway, all the leaves are gone and it's just gray... \n\nBut, oh, I'm sorry, I'm rambling again. What were we talking about? Oh, right, being underweight. Yes, I think I'd still feel the same. It's just... the feeling of being heavy, it doesn't go away just because a number on a scale changes. If I feel big, I feel big, and that's all there is to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your body shape and weight is accurate?\n\nPatient: Accurate? Well, I'm not sure what you mean by accurate, exactly. I know I sound like a broken record here, but like I was saying before, it really just comes down to how I feel in my clothes. If I put on my skirt for church and it’s digging into my waist, or if I accidentally see my profile in those awful store mirrors, my whole day is just ruined. I feel so huge, and I just know everyone is looking at me thinking I’ve completely let myself go. \n\nI actually bought that skirt at a little shop downtown that isn't even there anymore. It's a bakery now. Helen, the lady who opened it, used to be a teller at my bank. Just up and quit one day to bake sourdough! Can you imagine taking a risk like that at our age? Giving up a steady paycheck and a pension? My husband worked for the county for thirty-five years, and his pension has been an absolute godsend since he passed. He was always so careful with our finances. He kept this little green ledger in his breast pocket and wrote down every single penny we spent. Even if we just bought a newspaper or a pack of gum, out came the little green book... \n\nBut, oh, I'm sorry, I've lost the thread again. You were asking if I see myself accurately. I suppose I don't know what's accurate to other people and what isn't. I just know that when I look in the mirror and feel heavy, it feels completely real to me, and that's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still make yourself throw up after eating if you didn't feel like you were getting fat?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose not. The whole reason I do it is because of the weight. That's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight, and... well, it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening more often. \n\nIt reminds me of when my sister, Carol, started hoarding those porcelain thimbles. At first, she just bought one on a trip to Branson, Missouri. We went there for a long weekend, took a bus tour. The driver was a hoot, he kept telling these awful knock-knock jokes the whole way down. We stayed at a motel that had a pool shaped like a guitar, which I thought was a bit tacky, but Carol loved it. Anyway, she bought one thimble with a little painted cardinal on it. Next thing I knew, she had shelves and shelves of them, hundreds of them, all over her living room. She couldn't stop buying them. Her husband threatened to throw them all out, but he never did. He was all bark and no bite, that man. \n\nBut... oh, goodness, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm sorry. You were asking about the throwing up. Yes, if I didn't feel like I was getting fat, I wouldn't do it. It's just... I eat too much, and then I panic about gaining weight, and I just have to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to control your weight if you didn't throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I think I would. The throwing up is just... it's what I do because I feel so awful about my weight. Even if I stopped doing that, I'd still be constantly worried about how I look. Like I keep saying, it just takes one little thing—if my skirt feels tight before church, or if I catch my side view in one of those horrible store mirrors, it can just ruin my whole day. I feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go.\n\nSpeaking of church, Pastor Thomas gave a sermon last week about gluttony, and I just wanted to sink into the floor. He's a wonderful speaker, though. He used to be a youth minister down in Florida before he came to us. His wife, Sarah, plays the organ, and she has the most beautiful singing voice. They have three boys, all teenagers now, and you should see the grocery bills they must have! They had a potluck after service last month, and those boys went through three pans of baked ziti in about ten minutes. I made my famous green bean casserole, the one with the crispy onions on top. It’s my mother’s recipe, actually. She used to make it for every holiday. Thanksgiving, Christmas, Easter... she even made it for the Fourth of July once, which I thought was a bit odd for a summer barbecue, but everyone ate it anyway. \n\nBut... oh dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. What was the question? Oh, controlling my weight. Yes, I'd still feel the need to control it. The fear of getting fat is always there, no matter what else is going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your desire to throw up after eating is more about preventing weight gain or about changing how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Well, I think it's both, really, but mostly it's about the weight. Like I mentioned before, what finally scared me into coming in here is that after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight. And it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore, you know? It's just a terrible habit now.\n\nHabits are so hard to break, aren't they? My husband, bless his heart, used to smoke a pipe. He had this beautiful cherry wood pipe that he bought at a little tobacconist shop in Chicago when we went there for our honeymoon. We took the train up, the sleeper car. It was so glamorous back then, having dinner in the dining car with the white tablecloths and the silver service. The porter was this lovely man named George who brought us extra pillows. Anyway, Arthur would sit on the back porch every evening after dinner and smoke that pipe. The smell of cherry tobacco always reminds me of him. He tried to quit so many times. He tried chewing gum, he tried those little patches, he even tried hypnosis once! The hypnotist had this swinging pocket watch, just like in the movies. Arthur said it made him feel silly, but he didn't smoke for a whole week after that. \n\nOh, listen to me, rattling on about Arthur's pipe. I'm sorry, Doctor. But yes, to answer your question, the throwing up is definitely about not gaining weight from all the food. I just can't stand the thought of the scale going up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to throw up after eating if you could lose weight without doing so?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose if there were some magic pill to just make the weight fall off, I wouldn't feel the need to do it. But there isn't, is there? That's the whole problem. Like I was saying, what finally scared me into making this appointment is that after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight. And it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. It's a regular occurrence. \n\nIt's a bit like how I used to try every fad diet in the magazines. Oh, I remember the Grapefruit Diet back in the seventies. My friend Linda and I did that together. We bought crates of grapefruits from this little farm stand out on Route 9. The man who ran it, Mr. Henderson, always wore these bright red suspenders and a straw hat. He had a three-legged dog named Buster that used to sleep under the cash register. Buster lost his leg in a tractor accident, poor thing, but he hopped around just fine. Anyway, Linda and I ate grapefruit for breakfast, lunch, and dinner. My lips were so puckered from all the acid I could barely talk! Linda ended up quitting after three days because she said it gave her terrible heartburn, but I stuck it out for two weeks. I didn't lose an ounce, of course. \n\nOh, goodness, there I go again, talking about Buster the dog instead of answering your question. I'm so sorry. But yes, if I could just lose the weight normally, I wouldn't be throwing up. It's just the only way I know how to fix it after I've eaten too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your desire to be thinner is more about achieving a certain weight or about achieving a certain body shape?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it's really about both, isn't it? The weight and the shape. It's just... like I was saying earlier, it's those little things that set me off. If my skirt feels tight before church, or if I happen to catch my side view in one of those horrible, unflattering store mirrors... oh, it can just ruin my whole day. I immediately feel like everyone can tell I’ve let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of store mirrors, the ones at the department store where I work are just the absolute worst. I'm in the home goods section, mostly selling bedding and towels. We just got this new line of Egyptian cotton sheets in last week. They're a 800 thread count, very luxurious, but they only come in these terribly drab colors—sage green, taupe, and this dusty blue that reminds me of my Aunt Martha's old living room curtains. Aunt Martha lived in this drafty old Victorian house in upstate New York. She had this enormous grandfather clock in the hallway that chimed every quarter hour, day and night. You could never get a wink of sleep when you visited! She also had a collection of porcelain thimbles from all over the world, even though she didn't sew a stitch. She bought one in Paris once, on a trip with her bridge club...\n\nOh, dear me. I've completely lost the thread again, haven't I? Aunt Martha's thimbles have nothing to do with my weight. I'm so sorry, Doctor. But yes, to answer you... it's the shape, the way the clothes fit, the number on the scale... it all just makes me feel so terribly self-conscious.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating, or is it more about how your body looks to you?\n\nPatient: Well, I think they're really tied together, aren't they? The weight and the look of it all. Like I was mentioning before, it's just so upsetting when my skirt feels tight before church, or if I happen to catch my side view in one of those awful store mirrors. It just ruins my whole day because I immediately feel like everyone can tell I’ve let myself go. \n\nIt’s just like that time I bought this lovely floral dress for the church picnic. The picnic is always out at Centennial Park, by the big gazebo. They have the most wonderful potluck. Mrs. Higgins always brings her famous potato salad. She uses red potatoes and just a hint of dill, and it’s always the first thing to go. Last year, the pastor’s son, Tommy, tried to eat a whole watermelon by himself and ended up getting a terrible stomach ache. They had to lay him out on a picnic blanket under the big oak tree for an hour. That oak tree must be a hundred years old, easily. The roots come right up out of the ground, and you have to be so careful not to trip over them when you're carrying your plate...\n\nOh, goodness, I’m talking about potato salad and oak trees now. I do apologize, Doctor. But yes, to get back to what you asked... whether it's the weight or how I look... it's all of it. When my clothes don't fit right or I see myself looking heavy, I just panic, and that's why I end up throwing up. I just can't bear the thought of people looking at me and thinking I've gotten fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to throw up after eating if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or reflections?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose if I couldn't see myself... maybe it would help a little bit with the mirrors. But like I said, it's not just the mirrors. If my skirt feels tight before church, I still feel it, don't I? I can feel the waistband digging in, and it just ruins my whole day because I know everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of church, we have a new choir director, Mr. Peterson. He's very strict about the robes. He insists they all be pressed perfectly every Sunday. He used to direct a choir up in Boston, and he always talks about how professional they looked. His wife, Brenda, plays the organ, but she has arthritis in her hands, poor dear. She wears these copper bracelets that she swears by. She ordered them from a catalog that also sells these little ceramic birdhouses. I bought one shaped like a teapot for my garden, hoping to attract some finches. I put out thistle seed, but all I get are those pesky squirrels. They figured out how to hang upside down from the shepherd's hook and just empty the feeder in an afternoon...\n\nOh, dear, I'm off in the garden now, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What I mean to say is, even without mirrors, I can still feel when my clothes are too tight, and that's enough to make me panic and feel like I need to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel fat if you couldn't see yourself at all?\n\nPatient: Well, I think I would. I mean, even if I couldn't see my reflection at all, I'd still have to get dressed, wouldn't I? And like I was saying, if my skirt feels tight before church, or if my slacks are pinching at the waist, it just... it ruins my whole day. I can just feel it, and I immediately feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nIt's just like when I try to wear my winter coat. I bought this beautiful wool blend coat from the Macy's catalog three years ago. It has these lovely tortoiseshell buttons down the front. But the bottom button always feels a bit snug now. Speaking of buttons, my grandmother used to keep a giant tin of them. It was an old Royal Dansk butter cookie tin—you know the blue ones? We always thought there would be cookies inside, but it was just hundreds of buttons! She had pearl ones, brass ones from old military uniforms, and these tiny little glass ones that looked like candies. She used to string them on heavy thread to make necklaces for us when we were little girls. My sister Susan swallowed one once, a little red plastic one. My mother was frantic, absolutely beside herself. She called Dr. Evans, who used to make house calls back then. He drove this enormous boat of a car, an Oldsmobile, I think it was, and he always smelled faintly of peppermint and rubbing alcohol...\n\nOh, goodness me, I'm talking about Susan swallowing buttons now! I really do apologize, Doctor, my mind just wanders. But yes, to answer your question... even without seeing myself, if my clothes feel tight, I still feel fat. I just know it's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to control your weight if you didn't have the ability to purge after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, if I couldn't do that... I honestly don't know what I'd do. Because right now, after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight. It's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. I even do it at work. \n\nThe bathrooms at the department store where I work—I'm a sales associate in the misses clothing department—the plumbing in our employee restroom is just dreadful. We have to call Earl, the maintenance man, at least twice a week. Earl is such a sweet man, though. He has this droopy-eyed basset hound named Buster that he brings around to the loading dock on weekends. Buster actually won a blue ribbon at the county fair last summer for 'Best Trick'—he can balance a Milk-Bone on his nose for a full minute before he eats it! The county fair is always so crowded, though. We went last year, and they had this new booth selling deep-fried Oreos. Can you imagine? Frying a cookie! They smelled heavenly, but the line was a mile long, right past the big Ferris wheel. I haven't ridden a Ferris wheel since I was a teenager... I always get so dizzy when it stops at the very top and rocks back and forth...\n\nOh, dear me, I'm going on about Ferris wheels and dogs now. I am so sorry, Doctor, my brain just runs away with me. But yes, to answer you... if I couldn't do it, I'd be terrified of the weight. Because making myself throw up... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening all the time, and that's what really scared me into coming in.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, it's definitely about the weight gain. That's the main thing. Like I said, what finally scared me into coming in to see you is that after I eat too much, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom just so I won't gain weight. It's gotten to where it isn't just a one-time mistake anymore, you know? 

Speaking of mistakes, I made a terrible one with my azaleas last spring. I bought this new fertilizer from the garden center—the one over on Route 9, next to that diner that makes the wonderful blueberry pancakes. They serve them with real maple syrup in those little glass pitchers with the metal thumb-flaps. My husband used to love those pitchers. He collected vintage glassware for a while, mostly depression glass. He had a whole cabinet full of the green kind, the uranium glass that glows under a blacklight. We went to this antique show in the armory once to look for pieces, and it was so dusty in there I sneezed for three days straight. My nose was as red as a cherry tomato...

Oh, goodness, I'm talking about antique shows and pancakes now! I do apologize, Doctor. But yes, it's the weight gain. I'm just so afraid of putting on weight, and that's why I've been making myself sick.

I just can't bear the thought of people looking at me and thinking I've gotten fat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to throw up after eating if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or reflections?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose if I couldn't see myself... maybe it would help a little bit with the mirrors. But like I said, it's not just the mirrors. If my skirt feels tight before church, I still feel it, don't I? I can feel the waistband digging in, and it just ruins my whole day because I know everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nSpeaking of church, we have a new choir director, Mr. Peterson. He's very strict about the robes. He insists they all be pressed perfectly every Sunday. He used to direct a choir up in Boston, and he always talks about how professional they looked. His wife, Brenda, plays the organ, but she has arthritis in her hands, poor dear. She wears these copper bracelets that she swears by. She ordered them from a catalog that also sells these little ceramic birdhouses. I bought one shaped like a teapot for my garden, hoping to attract some finches. I put out thistle seed, but all I get are those pesky squirrels. They figured out how to hang upside down from the shepherd's hook and just empty the feeder in an afternoon...\n\nOh, dear, I'm off in the garden now, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What I mean to say is, even without mirrors, I can still feel when my clothes are too tight, and that's enough to make me panic and feel like I need to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel fat if you couldn't see yourself at all?\n\nPatient: Well, I think I would. I mean, even if I couldn't see my reflection at all, I'd still have to get dressed, wouldn't I? And like I was saying, if my skirt feels tight before church, or if my slacks are pinching at the waist, it just... it ruins my whole day. I can just feel it, and I immediately feel like everyone can tell I've let myself go. \n\nIt's just like when I try to wear my winter coat. I bought this beautiful wool blend coat from the Macy's catalog three years ago. It has these lovely tortoiseshell buttons down the front. But the bottom button always feels a bit snug now. Speaking of buttons, my grandmother used to keep a giant tin of them. It was an old Royal Dansk butter cookie tin—you know the blue ones? We always thought there would be cookies inside, but it was just hundreds of buttons! She had pearl ones, brass ones from old military uniforms, and these tiny little glass ones that looked like candies. She used to string them on heavy thread to make necklaces for us when we were little girls. My sister Susan swallowed one once, a little red plastic one. My mother was frantic, absolutely beside herself. She called Dr. Evans, who used to make house calls back then. He drove this enormous boat of a car, an Oldsmobile, I think it was, and he always smelled faintly of peppermint and rubbing alcohol...\n\nOh, goodness me, I'm talking about Susan swallowing buttons now! I really do apologize, Doctor, my mind just wanders. But yes, to answer your question... even without seeing myself, if my clothes feel tight, I still feel fat. I just know it's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to control your weight if you didn't have the ability to purge after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, if I couldn't do that... I honestly don't know what I'd do. Because right now, after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won’t gain weight. It's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. I even do it at work. \n\nThe bathrooms at the department store where I work—I'm a sales associate in the misses clothing department—the plumbing in our employee restroom is just dreadful. We have to call Earl, the maintenance man, at least twice a week. Earl is such a sweet man, though. He has this droopy-eyed basset hound named Buster that he brings around to the loading dock on weekends. Buster actually won a blue ribbon at the county fair last summer for 'Best Trick'—he can balance a Milk-Bone on his nose for a full minute before he eats it! The county fair is always so crowded, though. We went last year, and they had this new booth selling deep-fried Oreos. Can you imagine? Frying a cookie! They smelled heavenly, but the line was a mile long, right past the big Ferris wheel. I haven't ridden a Ferris wheel since I was a teenager... I always get so dizzy when it stops at the very top and rocks back and forth...\n\nOh, dear me, I'm going on about Ferris wheels and dogs now. I am so sorry, Doctor, my brain just runs away with me. But yes, to answer you... if I couldn't do it, I'd be terrified of the weight. Because making myself throw up... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening all the time, and that's what really scared me into coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your desire to throw up after eating is more about changing your body shape or preventing weight gain?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely about the weight gain. That's the main thing. Like I said, what finally scared me into coming in to see you is that after I eat too much, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom just so I won't gain weight. It's gotten to where it isn't just a one-time mistake anymore, you know? \n\nSpeaking of mistakes, I made a terrible one with my azaleas last spring. I bought this new fertilizer from the garden center—the one over on Route 9, next to that diner that makes the wonderful blueberry pancakes. They serve them with real maple syrup in those little glass pitchers with the metal thumb-flaps. My husband used to love those pitchers. He collected vintage glassware for a while, mostly depression glass. He had a whole cabinet full of the green kind, the uranium glass that glows under a blacklight. We went to this antique show in the armory once to look for pieces, and it was so dusty in there I sneezed for three days straight. My nose was as red as a cherry tomato...\n\nOh, goodness, I'm talking about antique shows and pancakes now! I do apologize, Doctor. But yes, it's the weight gain. I'm just so afraid of putting on weight, and that's why I've been making myself sick."
  }
